Today, we are pleased to note that free school meals are also going to be available in upper secondary school. At the same time, we have to say that the need for larger portions of food in high school forces us to calculate what we actually get for 78 cents. Certainly, at least the amount indicated by local authorities should be taken into account: school food actually costs about EUR 1 and a half on average. Last night, however, we saw on the news that a parish had set a precedent, stating that if a parent does not pay an extra 57 cents to 78 cents, his child would not receive school food. Their interpretation in Saku municipality is as simple as that. This is a dangerous precedent, because the state will either outsource or actually, as taxpayers, we will all allocate 78 cents for each child and there is no way to say that if a parent cannot or does not want to pay it a little less than half of the money, his or her child will not have a school night class thing. The first step is that we urgently need to think through all the possibilities that school food is really free, so that we can at least cover the cost of food. According to the local authorities, one portion, together with the wages of the caterers, costs around EUR one and a half, but with only food costs, the minimum cost is one euro. But on the other hand, we must certainly also analyse the possibilities of ensuring that the same drop reaches each child. Perhaps there are more and more municipalities who say that if the state does not give them more money or if the parent does not cover part of the cost of lunch, the child cannot eat. Our goal of reaching each child will then not be achieved. The second issue, which has also provided a great deal of discussion in recent days, is the decision of the Supreme Court on the financing of private education. In fact, a few years ago, as an amendment to the Basic School and Upper Secondary School Act, many local authorities were suddenly obliged to pay the so-called cart money, i.e. to cover the costs associated with the private school building or its rental. It also triggered a rather serious dispute in the courts. In particular, some local authorities considered that this was not justified because the local government has very little opportunity to speak in the issuing of training permits, in the management of their schools or in the supervision of private education. The Supreme Court has now also said that such an obligation in the Basic School and Upper Secondary School Act is unconstitutional. This provision needs to be reviewed as it cannot be implemented in this form. Now we're facing a dilemma. We are discussing the 2015 budget. As a minimum, 6.3 million additional costs should be included in next year's expenditure, but as the Commission's rapporteur emphasised today, the actual costs are over 13 million. These potential costs, which must be compensated for afterwards by local authorities, have not been taken into account, as the Supreme Court made very clear. The worst thing about solving this whole problem is definitely not doing anything. When we arrive at the third reading of the draft budget for 2015 and the needs for private education are not reflected there in either the education or financial reserves, we will have a strange situation. There are no obligations for local authorities in this form, as the provision has been declared unconstitutional. At the same time, the new measure is not designed and the victims are the same children who acquire private education at the choice of their parents, given that they are able to receive additional funding. There must certainly not be a situation in the education system where we have been having major disputes over a number of years, but still gave a message to private schools that they are very welcome in the Estonian educational landscape, but at the same time we leave private schools and children in them actually learning in the rain. We have the opportunity to quickly improve the situation here in the Riigikogu. We still have this opportunity today before the budget for next year is adopted. It is also possible to rapidly amend and supplement the basic school and upper secondary school law. There is certainly still a need to discuss the issue of school food before the third reading of the draft budget and, however, to find additional money to prevent such precedents from actually receiving free school food. We will, of course, thank the local authorities here who have managed to make the necessary money. However, as far as private education is concerned, we must certainly be able to ensure that children studying in private schools and private education do not suffer from a lack of money at all. After all, a large part of private schools deal with aspects of education that are not pursued or otherwise pursued by ordinary municipal or public education. We have a lot of special-need schools in private education, very small schools with alternative pedagogical approaches, classes aimed at children with emotional disorders, etc. These children should not suffer under any circumstances. I will limit myself to these two proposals here. Of course, educational issues cannot be overcome by autopsies or by teacher issues, by kindergarten issues, but since eight minutes is a very short time, I will limit myself to the two most topical issues. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U368	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The State Budget Act, which we are discussing today, which organises the life of 2015, has received its own resounding and praising names from its authors. Yes, there is certainly something positive about this, but it must not be forgotten that it is also a budget of word-breakingness. It is precisely in this budget that there should have been additional money for the maternity pension, but there is not. This budget thus confirms the unique situation in re-independence Estonia, where the pension previously promised by law is reduced. After all, in 2012 we passed a law in the Riigikogu, which gave more value to the work of raising children and provided for an additional pension for one parent of the family, regardless of when the child was born. So far, we had a strange situation in which the supplementary pension was calculated only for children born during the occupation. Under the law adopted, all children started to be taken into account in January 2013, and current pensioners receive an additional pension for each child for two years of retirement, i.e. approximately EUR 10 per month. As a general rule, it was decided that this supplementary pension would be paid by the mother, since she had been absent from work for some time as a result of raising her children and therefore received a lower pension. However, the supplementary pension may also be granted to the father, and in any case the pension supplement should be converted into the name of the father after the mother has left the earthly life. The current Act also received a letter that, as of 2015, each child will receive an additional pension in the currency of one more retirement year. This promise, which would concern 226000 pensioners next year, was already made in 2012 and, of course, we also included the money that would be spent there in the country's budget strategy. However, we are now facing a situation where reform parties and social democrats decided to postpone the payment of the pension supplement to parents for three years and thus reduce the pensions promised. This means that an average family who has raised two children takes away 120 euros a year. For the three years of the deferral period, this means, on average, EUR 360 of the money taken from the family. The representatives of the coalition have pointed out that the amount is not large and its withdrawal does not significantly affect the ability of pensioners to cope. Come on! Is it worth deceiving pensioners and breaking the word given by the state because of this so-called small money?! It is noteworthy that we were able to avoid reducing pensions even in the worst of times of economic crisis. However, just now that the major crisis is over and wages are slowly rising, the current government union has decided to deceive pensioners and to withdraw a total of EUR 86 million from the pensions that they have been promised in three years. I would like to remind you that this step of cheating on people was considered by the Socats and Reform Party to be so important, even the main issue facing the government, that at the beginning of the summer you linked the draft law on parental pension deferral with the government's vote of confidence. It is particularly obvious that the government asked to be trusted in order to be able to deceive pensioners. I do not consider it possible to support the state budget, which finally fixed the country's treachery to more than 260000 pensioners from whom this additional money was taken away. This is an disrespectful attitude toward the most important work our parents have done to raise children. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U372	Good ambassadors! I know most of you are watching this debate watching TV in your office, and that's why it's not so bad to talk to an empty hall right now. I want to talk about one specific issue concerning the state budget. In particular, the Fatherland and Res Publica Association have tabled Amendment 16, according to which we want to allocate EUR 300000 for the drafting and implementation of the legislation necessary for the creation of a single parent maintenance fund. This amount would increase the labour and management costs of the Ministry of Social Affairs. At the same time, it would be possible to prepare, in addition to the law, a part of the implementation and to ensure the necessary information to ensure that this maintenance fund is put into effect. I did not come here to waste your time on the floor, but you could, along with your hard work, for a moment before voting on the amendments, focus on what I am saying. Indeed, the chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs here defended the budget for the following year for quite a long time. However, he misunderstood the content of the above proposal. The Chairman of the Monetary Commission realized that for this EUR 300000 this fund is intended to be launched next year, but the content of the IRL proposal is that we have given a draft to the Riigikogu procedure, which will instruct the Government of the Republic to develop legislation on this fund during the next year and to implement it from 1 January 2016. A similar draft was handed over here by the Social Democratic Party Group, but after the new coalition, they probably decided to withdraw it under the pressure of the coalition partner. After the IRL Group re-submitted a similar draft, both the Social Democrats and the Centre Party announced that they would support this draft. This means that the Riigikogu will discuss this draft before Christmas, hopefully, but perhaps at the beginning of the new year. This is a one-reading draft on a single decision, and it will be adopted here if all political parties stick to their positions. As a result, the government would be obliged to draft a maintenance fund next year and implement it from 1 January 2016. The Chairman of the Monetary Commission indicated that these costs could not be one-off. We cannot foresee, as proposed by the IRL amendment, a reduction of EUR 300000 in the government reserve. Reserves cannot be spent for this purpose as they can be spent on one-off costs. Chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs! The content of the IRL amendment is that a one-off budget allocation is made so that the government can draw up a draft maintenance fund on the basis of the decision of the Riigikogu and prepare it to be implemented on 1 January 2016. During the debate on the 2016 budget next autumn, however, we need to provide the relevant amounts according to how the government has planned the day-to-day operation of the maintenance fund. Then we need to find a source of cover, which is not a one-off but a continuous one, ensuring that the fund functions properly. Why do you need a living fund? We have understood this by all of us who are in this Chamber, perhaps except for the Reform Party. In fact, one in four children in Estonia lives with a single parent. This was revealed in the public poll. To date, the amount of maintenance claims is already more than EUR 10 million, and this is the amount owed to specific children by parents who do not pay maintenance payments. And our duty as representatives of society is to ensure that these children receive monthly allowance. In other words, the fund would take over those claims for money assigned to another parent and collect these amounts using state coercive and motivational methods. That is why I appeal to you with a very specific request before we vote on the amendments. Amendment 16 was not supported by the Committee on Finance. From the point of view of the chairman of the Monetary Commission, we have also heard the argument that this is a source of cover, since only one-off costs could be covered from the government reserve fund. However, since the Committee on Monetary Affairs misunderstood that the amount intended to prepare a living fund is continuous, annual, it was therefore not supported. In fact, the Chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs is right: from the government reserve we can allocate money to one-off costs. But this concrete proposal, which has been put forward by the IRL Group and which has also been supported in words by the Social Democrats and the Centre Party Group, envisages a one-off expenditure. And if you, dear members of the Riigikogu, are voting on this amendment, please do not react automatically to the words that the IRL Group has proposed this! Do not press the red button or the green button immediately, but think intently about what I have just said before voting on Amendment 16. We can actually also provide a financial cover for this decision, which we will most likely accept this year if you all keep your promises. That was the point of my speech. I hope it got through the TV channel to your offices, where you're all staying and listening to what's being discussed here. I would like to remind you once again that I was talking about Amendment 16, which concerns the amount of legislation on the maintenance fund and its implementation, which would be paid out of the government reserve once. As you understand, the total amount of the state budget is EUR 300000, a very small amount. Thank you.

Nor am I comforted by the statements of the coalition's top politicians that the European Commission does not know how to calculate, but we have good accountants here. With the new basic law on the state budget adopted in spring, we have established that the state budget must be structurally balanced or in surplus. Several experts have come to the conclusion that the structural budget surplus for the years 2014 to 2015 has been estimated to be higher than in reality in the Estonian Government. Therefore, the IRL proposed a reduction of EUR 10 million in government expenditure in the budget to help improve the balance of the state budget and to make public administration more effective. It is sad that the party that led the government for years has become a brake on Estonia's development. Perhaps the best example is administrative reform. Years ago, people were intimidated that when the administrative reform was implemented, villages would run empty. It's been years now, there's no administrative reform, but the villages are still empty. At last, the general public, as well as most members of the government and the Riigikogu, as well as most political parties, have realised that administrative reform is inevitable. There's no point burning money with a big flame. In order for progress to be made, directors-visions are needed to determine the direction of the process. We can see the same picture in our small Estonian society, as we are clinged to the balance between incomes and expenses. All this is all the more strange that agriculture relies on local resources and is still one of the most national industries in Estonia. I am therefore extremely sorry that the coalition has completely turned its back on the farmer, the food industry and the rural economy. I will bring some vivid facts to make sure that the story does not remain slogand and general. Transitional support from the state budget is not paid. We are the only one in the Baltic region, and perhaps the only one among the countries of the European Union as a whole, who has such a right and who does not exercise it. The fact that this support was not meant to be large in this form last year is not sufficient. Last year there was no such situation on the world market, no sanctions stemming from political decisions were applied and the market lost because of them, there had not been a swine plague in Estonia and there were not many other problems that, by cumulation, have caused a crisis in this sector. And we are the only country in the European Union that does not allocate a single cent from its budget to alleviate this crisis and does not take any special measures! On the contrary, apart from the fact that we do not pay transitional aid, we do not pay special aid for pig farming. We will reduce the European Union's direct payments by EUR 70 million over the whole period. We will reduce the financing of applied agricultural research by around 40% compared to this year's budget. Regional funding will shrink by around 15% next year compared to this year. When we co-finance the rural development plan, we are the most modest among the countries of the European Union. It is likely that the budget for next year will be the only budget in the last ten years where no cents of money will be allocated to the movement of the Kodukant. At least after second reading, we must say that this is the case in the budget. Moreover, in addition to what is not in the budget, there is also concern about what is in this budget from the point of view of agriculture, rural development and food production. In fact, in this budget, agricultural subsidies for natural persons are taxed at 20% of income tax in the history of re-independence Estonia for the first time, which means that the subsidies will decrease by 20%. This puts the natural persons most of the beneficiaries in a worse position than companies and self-employed persons. Income tax is also levied on compensation for loss of income. I repeat once again: income tax is levied on loss of income benefits, as well as 20%. Indeed, this budget is the most anti-farm and rural budget in this century. And I am extremely sorry that social democrats have been involved in putting together such a budget, who in the past have said a great deal about the need to develop rural, agricultural and food production. I am not surprised that the Reform Party, with whom we have been in the coalition for a long time, is indifferent. One of the reasons for the coalition's crackdown was certainly that we were unable to find consensus on the issues of agriculture and rural financing. But the Social Democrats, as I repeat, the chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs here today said that the goals of Estonian agriculture and rural development were abandoned in the name of going to the coalition. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U405	Thank you. I now request the speech chair of the Riigikogu, Kadri Simson! Eight minutes.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U406	Ladies and gentlemen, Good chairman of the sitting! I have listened attentively to today's speeches; many of the previous speakers can be accepted. In quite a number of speeches, criticism was that the budget for the coming year does not have a big target and that Estonia's life will not be passed on. But what were we waiting for? In spring, an interim government was set up, which extended the election campaign to one year, while at the same time certainly did not have the mandate to make important decisions. The Interim Government has presented us with the budget for the election year, and on many points that can be criticised in this budget, it is simply an attempt to live up to the electoral promises late. This is quite a number of years when the state budget is brought before the Riigikogu in a situation where costs are more than income. One big question is whether next year may be the year in which the Reform Party implements its promise made several years ago, eliminating more than 70 million euros of tax revenue from the Estonian state budget. I would remind you that the 2007 Coalition Agreement already promised that by 2011 the income tax rate in Estonia would be 18%, but then it was realised that there would also have to be revenue coming from somewhere in order to carry out the tasks assigned to the state and that the reduction of income tax already legalised was postponed. In the coming year, 1% of income tax will eventually be reduced and it will take away quite a large amount from the budget. In addition to having more expenditure than revenue, quite a number of tasks have not been fulfilled in the budget. One of them is the promise to raise police and rescue workers' salaries. If we look at a budget that has been presented more precisely for the second reading, we will not see an increase of 4.5% in the salary fund in the budget of the Border Guard and Rescue Board. One amendment proposed by the Centre Party is to give the money needed to raise the salary in this area so that the pay of police officers should not rise at the cost of concentrating their colleagues. Another obvious problem caused by the lack of money is tax increases. We all remember quite a few tax increases were made during the summer. One of them will bring a 38% increase in room temperature to people who heat their homes with light fuel oil next year. Already knowing that our proposal to change the budget cannot be understood, the Central Party Group launched a draft law on 3 November, the content of which will be to cancel the increase in excise duty on light fuel oil next year. This excise duty affects not only homeowners but also people and municipalities living in apartment buildings. A lot of kindergartens use that fuel. As a general rule, only one million additional revenues from the tax increase that will fall on the municipalities will be planned for next year. This amendment was also rejected by the Riigikogu in the Committee on Monetary Affairs, although the people of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and the Ministry of Finance were also present in the Committee on Monetary Affairs, as was the Specialist of the Tax and Customs Board, who all admitted that there were a lot of questionable issues in this law. One question is that the law passed in summer states that everyone who has stocked light fuel oil for their boilers will have to pump out the tanks on 1 May and sell the fuel to whom it comes out of that tank. This law is a failure. I dare say that Tarmo Mänd, a member of the Reform Party Group, was amazed with a loud voice that who has accepted such foolishness. In the summer, sociers and reformists voted in favour of such foolishness. Now the question is how can this stupidity be corrected? Today, with regard to the budget, our amendment will be voted so that this revenue will not be included in the state budget, because it will not be received as such. True, there are also commendable things in the budget. We certainly support the fact that children's benefits will be raised next year. We've been talking about this for a long time. Our ideas that free school food will reach all students from 1st grade to the end of upper secondary school are also included in next year's budget. At the same time, a number of articles have appeared showing that 78 euro cents per meal is certainly not the amount for which all municipalities can offer free school lunch for children. The Central Party's proposal to increase the school food subsidy to EUR 1 per portion is now to be voted on. This would give relief, in particular, to those municipalities that are currently paying a considerable amount of money to get free school lunch for their children. But in places where the city or rural municipality government does not pay extra money to provide school meals, it would still give the children the opportunity to eat free of charge. The Committee on Finance also discussed whether it would be possible to introduce free bus rides on land. The current situation is that two thirds of the money needed to keep the county lines running is obtained in the form of subsidies. All over Estonia, it would only take 13 million euros to make the county lines free of tickets. This would give people living in the countryside an additional opportunity to find employment, perhaps not as high-paid as in cities, but, on the other hand, close to their home and to get moving without extra spending. The total amount required is relatively moderate on a national scale. If we want to bring a parallel, this amount will be as high as the cost to the city of Tallinn of the introduction of free public transport. If there is political will, then the Estonian state can certainly afford it. We would thus give the first clear indication that it is possible to limit peripheralisation. We would also signal that living in the countryside is not punished, that living in the country is no more expensive than living in the city. This brings me to another important amendment. Our proposal is that the Estonian state, like its southern neighbours, would support farmers at the promised rate. We all know that Estonia's agricultural subsidies are smaller anyway than the average in the European Union, and we also miss the opportunity to pay our own subsidies. This amendment will also be voted on today, and I ask for the support of the other groups. Finally, I would like to say that it is very strange if no source of cover offered by the opposition is ever suitable, be it a reserve, be it tax changes, be it the transfer of an object elsewhere. In order to understand how bad this behaviour is, every party must probably be in opposition from time to time. The IRL will understand how such an attitude will work when dealing with this draft. I wish the Reform Party strength next year when you are in this situation! Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U482	Representatives of the initiator of the draft, the Deputy Secretary-General of Financial Policy and External Relations of the Ministry of Finance Märten Ross and the Head of the Department of Enterprise and Accounting Policy of Kurmet Ojamaa and the President of the Auditor Assembly Märt-Martin Development were invited to the meeting on 3 November. However, according to the Ministry, it is not possible to make very fundamental changes within the framework of this draft, which require further consideration and preparation. At the same time, without amending the existing law, it is possible to make some necessary adjustments, for example, a new Supervisory Board will already be appointed in March 2015. A longer discussion arose in connection with the proposal of the Auditor Board, according to which self-training could be included as part of the professional development required of the sworn auditor. The initiator did not support this proposal. Both had explained their views in writing to the Commission and this proposal was not supported by the Commission. All ten amendments submitted for the second reading of the draft are proposals from the commission, which were taken into account by the Commission on the basis of the reasons set out in the explanations of the amendments. The procedural decisions of the commission were as follows: to refer draft 613 to the plenary session for second reading on 19 November 2014, to propose the termination of the second reading and, when the second reading is closed, to send the draft to the third reading by 3 December 2014 with a proposal for a final vote. All decisions were taken by consensus.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U487	We will move on to agenda item 13 - the first reading of the Biocidal Products Act initiated by the Government of the Republic and of the Draft Act amending the State Fees Act 754. Urmas Kruuse, Minister of Health and Labour of the Riigikogu, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U488	Honourable President! Honourable members of the Riigikogu! I am pleased to present to you the draft law amending the Biocidal Products Act and the State Fees Act, which the Riigikogu has adopted on 20 October. The amendments to the Biocidal Products Act and the State Fees Act are intended to ensure the implementation of the European Union Biocidal Products Regulation. To this end, the current Biocidal Products Act will be aligned with the Biocidal Products Regulation. Biocides are necessary for the destruction or control of organisms harmful to human and animal health and to natural or manufactured products, in particular by chemical or biological means. However, because of their intrinsic properties, biocidal products may also pose a risk to humans, animals and the environment. It is therefore important that the risks associated with the use of biocidal products are controlled. Biocidal products should only be made available and used after their intrinsic properties have been identified, their risk to humans and the environment has been assessed, their efficacy in the target organism has been demonstrated, and guidance has been developed for their safe use as far as possible. To describe this, manufacturers shall draw up appropriate documentation, i.e. a dossier, for the biocidal product and its active substance. The accuracy and relevance of the dossier for both the active substance and the biocidal product shall be assessed by the competent authorities of the European Union or the Member States. Once the active substance of a biocidal product has been approved at European Union level, a dossier can be drawn up for a biocidal product containing that active substance. Once the biocidal product dossier has been found to comply with the requirements, authorisation for the making available and use of the biocidal product under the Biocidal Products Regulation shall be granted either at the level of the European Union or at the level of a Member State. Until 1 September 2013, the Biocidal Products Directive and several Commission implementing regulations regulated the marketing and use of biocidal products. Unfortunately, the Biocidal Products Directive was not implemented as expected. In 2008, it became apparent that a significant part of the directive's provisions had not yet been implemented in the Member States. Many of the provisions were unclear and unevenly applied and, in practice, put a heavy burden on some situations. In order to improve the situation, a new directly applicable legislation, a Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council, called the Biocidal Products Regulation, was developed in the European Union. The Biocidal Products Regulation entered into force in June 2012 and will apply from 1 September 2013. With the entry into force of the new directly applicable legislation, there was a need to improve the internal legal space. There is no need for duplication of national regulations with regard to the implementation of uniform European Union requirements. However, it is necessary to establish appropriate implementing measures for the application of the Biocidal Products Regulation and to ensure national monitoring of compliance with the requirements of the Regulation. The draft repeals a large part of the provisions of the Biocidal Products Act, leaving for a transitional period those which already issue registration certificates or marketing authorisations or issue new registration certificates. Registration certificates are issued during a transitional period, i.e. at a time when the active substance of the biocidal product has not yet been approved at European Union level, and therefore no proper dossier can be drawn up for the biocidal product and each country allows the biocidal product to be placed on the market in accordance with its own requirements. In Estonia, you should apply for a registration certificate for this purpose. The terms will also be clarified by aligning them with those used in the Regulation. The amendments to the draft can be divided into two parts. Firstly, for the implementation of the Biocidal Products Regulation, the competent authority, which is the Health Agency, is designated to carry out the procedural procedures laid down in the Regulation. Fees and fees shall be established for the procedural procedures carried out by the Health Board under the Biocidal Products Regulation. The monitoring of compliance with the requirements of the Biocidal Products Act and the Biocidal Products Regulation will be specified. Secondly, amendments are proposed which are not directly due to the implementation of the Biocidal Products Regulation. In order to reduce the potential health and environmental risks posed by biocidal products, it is necessary to promote the sustainable use of biocidal products. The draft specifies the requirements for the safe use of the biocidal product, especially for the making available and use of biocidal products for professional use. In this way, professional biocidal products may only be made available in wholesale trade and the trader must submit an economic notification. Provision shall be made for who is a professional user and what requirements are to be met. It is important to have the knowledge and skills that can be obtained through a variety of training courses. Of course, there are also requirements for training whose curriculum is based on the professional standards of the exterminator. Specify who is required to qualify for the fifth level of the pest-fighter's professional qualification as a professional pest-control service provider. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! This is a bill that helps to protect human health. I ask you to accept this law!

As we know, crises, including crises that require the response of the international community, are emerging rapidly and unexpectedly today. NATO or the European Union is also often forced to participate in the regulation of these crises. According to the International Military Cooperation Act of the Republic of Estonia, the use of our Defence Forces personnel in such operations requires a mandate from the Riigikogu, but upon the outbreak of the crisis, international organisations are forced to deploy competent staff officers quickly to the future mission area. And in order for Estonia to be able to participate quickly and flexibly in the planning of these missions from the beginning, we ask for a 'preventive mandate' for up to five members of the Defence Forces. Then we will have a flexible opportunity to immediately deploy Estonian staff officers to the mission area if necessary. This procedure was also used in 2013, when the European Union launched a crisis regulation operation in Mali, and this decision justified itself in every way. I would remind you that, in accordance with the International Military Cooperation Act, in the presence of such a proactive mandate, the Minister of Defence, in coordination with the Minister for Foreign Affairs, will be able to order the deployment of active troops. The President of the Republic, the Executive Board of the Riigikogu and the Chairman of the Parliament's National Defence Committee will also be informed of this arrangement without delay. Of course, we cannot rule out the need for such a posting in the next year. So I ask Parliament to support this decision.

Battlegroups are units within the European Union's multi-ethnic battalion tactical group, capable of rapid response. Estonia participates in the Nordic Battle Group with Norway, Finland, Sweden, Latvia, Lithuania and Ireland. According to the battle group concept, the battle groups are on 15-day standby, of which five days are reserved for the decision on deployment and 10 days for the deployment of the mission area. The concept is that battle groups must be able to stay on the mission for up to 120 days. The Nordic Battle Group is ready for the first half of next year. However, since at least theoretically there is a possibility that the battle group will be decided to activate and deploy the operation at the end of this preparation period, it is also justified to ask the Riigikogu for an annual mandate. Estonia has also been ready to react in the Nordic Battle Group in 2008 and 2011. We appreciate this concept very highly and are very grateful to Sweden, which is the leader of the Nordic Battle Group, and we have also supported the principle that the European Union should be prepared to actually use battle groups in these crisis management operations, which correspond to the idea and description of the concept of battle groups. The objective of the mission is to provide military and training advice to the Malian Armed Forces in order to be able to better protect Mali's territorial integrity and reduce organised crime and the threat of terrorism in the country. It is important for Estonia to contribute to the implementation of the European Union's common security and defence policy, and in 2015 up to 10 active Estonian troops will participate in the European Union training mission. Estonia's participation is the same this year. The UN peacekeeping mission MINUSMA was established in spring 2013 by a UN Security Council Resolution. The objective of this mission is to stabilise Mali and restore state power throughout the country, to support the protection of civilians and UN representatives, to protect human rights and to provide humanitarian assistance. By 2014, the Riigikogu authorised two active troops to participate in the MINUSMA mission. According to the draft, up to 10 active troops will participate in the mission in 2015. In addition to staff officers, the Reconnaissance Team will also contribute in 2015. The National Defence Committee discussed the draft before submitting it to first reading at its meeting on 11 November. Defence Minister Sven Mikser also took part in the meeting. On 4 November, the Defence Forces General Staff held a meeting of the National Defence Commission, where the Commission received an overview from representatives of the Defence Forces of the international missions taking part in the Estonian Defence Forces, including the situation in Mali. At its meeting on 11 November, the National Defence Committee decided by consensus to invite the Board of the Riigikogu to include the draft under discussion for the first reading on the agenda of the session of the Riigikogu on 19 November 2014. A speaker was appointed to serve as a waitress for the National Defence Committee. In addition, the National Defence Committee decided by consensus to invite the Riigikogu to conclude the first reading of the draft under discussion and to propose to the President of the Riigikogu that the draft be fixed by the deadline for submitting amendments. a 2 December at 1 p.m. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U527	The next item is the first reading of draft 763 of the Riigikogu's decision 'Use of the Defence Forces in fulfilling the international obligations of the Estonian State in the composition of NATO' by the Government of the Republic. Secretary of Defense Sven Mikser, please.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U528	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! Draft Decision 763 concerns the use of the Defence Forces in fulfilling the international obligations of the Estonian state in the NATO response forces. NATO's response force was established on the basis of the decision of the 2002 Prague historic summit of NATO. This is the same summit where Estonia was invited to join NATO. NATO's response force is a high-speed unit that must be able to conduct military operations worldwide and also ensure that NATO's rapid response capability is maintained and maintained. In 2015, Estonia wishes to contribute to the NRF with one mine hunter and a crew of up to 40 active troops, as well as up to five staff officers. All in all we ask for next year's mandate for up to 45 active troops. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U529	Thank you very much. Any questions for the waitress? There are no questions. I would like to ask Lauri Laas, Member of the National Defence Commission for a co-report!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U530	Madam President! Honourable Members of the Riigikogu! For more information on the general principles of the draft under discussion. Estonia's participation in NATO's response forces is due to NATO membership. All NATO Member States contribute to the NRF, except Iceland, which does not have its own defence forces. The NRF's response force is also the most important tool in the rapid launch of NATO's collective self-defence operations, if necessary. Therefore, participation in the NRF is also related to Estonia's security. NATO response teams may be used for a collective defence operation under the NATO Treaty as set out in Chapters VI and VII of the United Nations Charter. The use of the NRF is decided unanimously by the NATO Council, which means that the use of the NRF can only take place with the consent of Estonia. The National Defence Committee discussed the draft before submitting it to the first reading in its s. a at its meeting on 11 November. Mr Sven Mikser was present at this meeting as the representative of the author of the draft act. The National Defence Committee also visited s. On 4 November, in the framework of the departure session, the Defence Forces General Staff, where the Commission received an overview from representatives of the Defence Forces of the international missions taking part in the Estonian Defence Forces, including the preparation of the Estonian mine hunter, its staff and staff and staff officers and subordinate officers to participate in NATO's response forces. The National Defence Committee decided s. a At its meeting on 11 November, by consensus, the Board of the Riigikogu will be invited to include the draft under discussion for the first reading in the Riigikogu. On the agenda of the 19 November session, the Riigikogu was asked to conclude the first reading of the draft. The rapporteur was appointed here. In addition, the National Defence Committee decided by consensus to propose a draft deadline for the submission of amendments. a 2 December at 1 p.m. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U532	Our 19th item on the agenda today is the first reading of draft 764 of the Riigikogu's decision 'Extension of the term of use of the Defence Forces in the execution of the international obligations of the Estonian state in the post-conflict peace-building mission in Lebanon, Israel, Egypt and Syria' presented by the Government of the Republic. ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U533	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! Draft Decision 764 concerns the extension of the term of use of the Defence Forces in the execution of the international obligations of the Estonian State in the post-conflict peace-building mission in Lebanon, Israel, Egypt and Syria. This mission is known as UNTSO, short for United Nations Truce Supervision Organization. This is the oldest mission in the United Nations, which began in 1948. Estonia is also participating in this mission this year, and this year the government went to the Riigikogu, asking for our mandate to be increased from three Defence Forces to six, as three of the staff officers had already been filled with active troops and Estonia had the opportunity to contribute to one of the senior officers. That is why, in the middle of the year, we asked for the limit to be increased to six in order to ensure little flexibility. Today, we ask for an extension of the mandate of the Riigikogu to use up to six active troops in this operation during the next year. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U534	Any questions? There are no questions. Thank you very much, Secretary of Defense. Aivar Riisalu, Vice-Chairman of the National Defence Commission, would you like to speak with me?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U535	Good President! Ladies and gentlemen, Good minister! Indeed, this bill deals with one of Estonia's oldest and long-term positive missions. The National Defence Committee discussed the draft before submitting it to the first reading in its s. a at its meeting on 11 November. Mr Sven Mikser, Minister of Defence, represented the author of the draft, took part in the meeting. The draft is also related to the meeting of the National Defence Commission on 4 November at the General Staff of the Defence Forces, where the members of the committee received an overview from the representatives of the Defence Forces of the events taking place on international missions with the participation of Estonian Defence Forces. The mission is based on UN Security Council Resolution No 50 of 1948 and Estonian Defence Forces have been participating in it as observers and staff officers since 1997. This is a purely UN Blue helmet observation mission. As a member of the United Nations, Estonia contributes to peace in crisis regions. ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U537	Our last item today, item 20, reads as follows: The extension of the deadline for the use of the Defence Forces by the Riigikogu in fulfilling the international obligations of the Estonian state on the peace-building mission in Kosovo, first reading of the draft 765. Please report to Secretary of Defense Sven Mikser!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U538	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! The last draft mission is number 765 and its content is the extension of the term of use of the Defence Forces in fulfilling the international obligations of the Estonian state in the peace-building mission in Kosovo. NATO has been active in peace-building in Kosovo since 1999, when an operation known as KFOR (Kosovo Force) was launched to end ethnic cleansing in Kosovo and to create stability and peace. Estonia has already participated in this operation since 1999, and ten years ago it was the largest international operation for us where we participated. Since then, our contribution has gradually decreased, but NATO's action to prevent the revival of hostilities in Kosovo remains necessary. Currently, around 30 countries participate in the Kosovo operation with a total of around 5 000 people. Estonia's current contribution is up to three members of the Defence Forces and the government will also ask the Riigikogu for a mandate to use up to three active troops for the operation of Kosovo for the following year. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U539	Thank you very much. Any questions for the Minister? There are no questions. Ms Ene Ergma, Member of the National Defence Committee of the Chair, for a co-report.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-11-19_U540	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, According to this draft decision, Estonia will continue to contribute to KFOR, a NATO-led peace-building mission in Kosovo based on UN Security Council Resolution 12441999 of June. Estonia's contribution is up to three active troops, who serve at the KFOR headquarters in Priština. By contributing to the KFOR mission, Estonia fulfils the allied responsibilities arising from NATO membership. Kosovo's stability has a significant impact on the security of both the Western Balkans and Europe as a whole, and the aim of international forces, including Estonia, is to support Kosovo's government in ensuring security and stability in the country. The National Defence Committee discussed the draft before submitting it to first reading. a on 11 November, attended by Sven Mikser, Minister of Defence. On 4 November of this year, the National Defence Commission also held a meeting at the General Command of the Defence Forces, where the Commission received an overview from representatives of the Defence Forces of the international missions taking part in the Estonian Defence Forces, including the situation in Kosovo.
